\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{i}\atop\simeq}

3.2
Comparison map.

Now on the other hand, we get another oplax cocone over the composite
T\mathbb{A}:I\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
as the data of
(Tq_{i}:TA_{i}\rightarrow T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}A_{i})_{i\in I}
together with the
Tq_{d}:Tq_{j}Tf_{d}\Rightarrow Tq_{i}
as transition 2-cells at
d:i\rightarrow j
. This defines a universal morphism (which we could see as the comparison map measuring how far
T
is from preserving oplax bicolimits):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

together, for each
i
, with an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{i}\atop\simeq}

3.3
Codescent object at the oplax bicolimit: lower data.

Now, on one hand, we get two parallel 1-cells; first the composite of the multiplication at the oplax bicolimit with the image of the comparison map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}

and, one the other hand, the image of the oplax bicolimit of the structure maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}

We now construct a common pseudosection of those two parallel 1-cells. As well as the structure maps of the algebras provided us with a canonical 1-cell, their units altogether provide an oplax cocone
(q^{T}_{i}\eta_{A_{i}}:A_{i}\rightarrow{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}TA_{i})_{i\in I}
with transition 2-cell at
d:i\rightarrow j
provided by the pasting with the pseudonaturality component of
\eta
at the underlying map
f_{d}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{j}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tf_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{A_{j}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{f_{d}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{d}}

This oplax cocone provides again a map, in the reverse direction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}

Then, the pseudonaturality of
\eta
gives an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}\atop\simeq}

Lemma 3.4
.

The morphism
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}\eta_{A_{i}}
is a common section of
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}a_{i}
and
\mu_{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}A_{i}}Ts
.

Proof.

In each
i
of
I
, the structure map
a_{i}
defines a pseudo-retraction of the unit
\eta_{A_{i}}
thanks to the invertible 2-cell
\alpha^{t}_{i}
. Moreover the coherence condition at the transition morphisms
(f_{d},\phi_{d})

ensures that those pseudo-retractions are pseudo-natural: this defines a pseudo-retraction in the 2-functors category and pseudonatural squares
[I,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TU_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathbb{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}\atop\simeq}

whith
\overline{a}:TU_{T}\mathbb{A}\rightarrow U_{T}\mathbb{A}
defined by the pseudonaturality squares given by the data of the
a_{i}
and the
(f_{d},\phi_{d})
and
\overline{\alpha}
provided by the structures 2-cells
\alpha_{i}
. This pseudoretraction is sent by the 2-functor
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}:[I,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
to a pseudoretraction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{\alpha}\atop\simeq}

For the second retraction: first observe that the pseudomonad data
\zeta
gives at
{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}A_{i}
an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}\atop\simeq}

Let us now produce a decomposition of
\eta_{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}\;A_{i}}
through
{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}\eta_{\;A_{i}}
. The naturality of the oplax bicolimit at the arrows
\eta_{A_{i}}
provides us with invertible 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\theta}_{i}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}

which are oplax bicolimit inclusion in
[2,\mathcal{C}]_{{\bf ps}}
: they will be used to produce a pseudosquare form data
\eta_{A_{i}}\rightarrow 1_{T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}A_{i}}
in
[2,\mathcal{C}]_{{\bf ps}}
. Let us construct those data: recall that we exhibited at
3.2
an invertible 2-cell
\sigma_{i}
at each
i
; now paste them together with the naturality invertible 2-cells
\eta_{q_{i}}
of
\eta
at the oplax bicolimit inclusion
q_{i}
: then those data, which are moreover pseudonatural, induce uniquely an invertible 1-cell
\sigma=\langle\eta_{q_{i}}\sigma_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}

This provides us with a decomposition as below, which it suffices to paste with
\zeta_{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}A_{i}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\sigma\atop\simeq}

∎

3.6
Codescent object at the oplax bicolimit: higher coherence data.

The higher codescent data must encode composition-like operation. The higher object is the free on the oplax bicolimit over the iterated power of
T
: one must choose
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}TTA_{i}
. Then we claim that the three parallel higher maps are to be chosen as the following:

-

the first map is the image of the oplax colimits of the images of the structure map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}

-

the second map is the image of the multiplication

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}

-

the last one is the composite of the multiplication at the oplax colimit of the images along the image of the second comparison map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}

Lemma 3.7
.

The following diagram is a codescent object in
\mathcal{C}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\small{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TA_{i}}Tt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i}}Ts}

Proof.

We exhibited the lower coherence data corresponding to the
n_{0}
,
n_{1}
in
lemma

3.4
. Now let us construct the higher coherence data: we have to guess canonical invertible 2-cells between three combinations of lower and higher cells.

For the first 2-cell, corresponding to
\theta_{01}
of
1.4
, observe that the data of the
\alpha_{i}^{s}
form actually a square of pseudonatural transformation in
[I,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTU_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TU_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TU_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{T}\mathbb{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}^{s}\atop\simeq}

Applying the 2-functor
{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}:[I,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
returns then a canonical invertible 2-cell which we can take after applying
T
once more as the witness of the second coherence condition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\alpha^{s}_{i}}

The second higher 2-cell, corresponding to
\theta_{02}
, can be chosen as the following pasting, where
\theta
was introduced in
3.5
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\theta\atop\simeq}

For the last 2-cell corresponding to
\theta_{12}
, we have first to construct an auxiliary 2-cell relating the multiplication at the oplax bicolimit and the two comparison maps. We need a canonical cell comparing the action of
t
and the action of
s
. We can consider the following pasting, made of the pseudonaturality square of
\mu
at the inclusion
q_{i}
together with the comparisons 2-cells
\sigma_{i}
, its image along
T
and the 2-cell
\tau_{i}
we constructed at
3.5
for each
i
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{TT}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq^{T}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTq_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{i}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{q_{i}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\sigma_{i}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{i}^{-1}\atop\simeq}

Then using again the oplax bicolimit property in the 2-category
{\bf ps}[2,\mathcal{C}]
we induce a universal 2-cell
\tau=\langle\sigma_{i}^{-1}\mu_{q_{i}}T\sigma_{i}(\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}Ts)*\tau_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}
decomposing the 2-cell above as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{TT}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{T}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau\atop\simeq}

Then one can consider the following pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTs}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{s}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\tau\atop\simeq}

∎

3.8
Codescent object at the oplax bicolimit: pseudo-algebra structure.

Now we can lift the codescent object above in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
, for each object has an obvious free pseudo-algebra structure on it, while we can guess convenient pseudomorphism structures on the 1-cells:

-

for
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}\;a_{i}
take the pseudonaturality square of the multiplication:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}}\atop\simeq}

-

similarly, for
T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}
, take the pseudonaturality square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}}\atop\simeq}

-

for
\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}Ts
take the the pasting:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTs}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ts}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{s}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}

-

for
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}\mu_{A_{i}}
take the pseudonaturality square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TT{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}}}\atop\simeq}

-

for
\mu_{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}TA_{i}}Tt
take the pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TT{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTt}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Tt}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}\atop\simeq}

-

and finally for
T{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}Ta_{i}
take

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TT{A_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}}\atop\simeq}

Lemma 3.9
.

The following diagram, which we shall denote
\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{A}}
, is a codescent object in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TTA_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TTA_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TA_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\small{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\mu_{A_{i}})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;Ta_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;Ta_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TA_{i}}Tt,\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}*\mu_{Tt}\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;T{A_{i}}}*TTt)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;\eta_{A_{i}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;a_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;a_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i}}Ts,\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}*\mu_{s}\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}*TTs)}

Proof.

It is a tedious, yet straightforward manipulation of the coherence data of the pseudomonads to check the data of
3.8
define really pseudomorphisms; similarly for the verification that the underlying invertible 2-cell in the codescent structure of
lemma

3.7
and
lemma

3.4
satisfies the coherence conditions of transformation of pseudomorphisms of pseudo-algebras: we let this as an exercise for the careful reader.
∎

3.10
Morphism of codescent object over the inclusions.

Beware that oplax bicolimit inclusions
q_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}_{I}A_{i}
do not bear pseudomorphism structure; however, it is possible to induce from the data we constructed above a morphism of codescent object

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{X}_{(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i}))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{A}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{q_{i}}}

from the data we constructed throughout this section: its pseudonaturality squares will be the following:

-

at the lower data, take the following data (where
\theta_{i}
as defined at
3.1
and
\mu_{i}
at

):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TTA_{i},\mu_{TA_{i}},(\xi_{TA_{i}},\rho_{TA_{i}}))}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}))}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\theta_{i}\atop\simeq}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TTA_{i},\mu_{TA_{i}},(\xi_{TA_{i}},\rho_{TA_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;TA_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;TA_{i}}))}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu_{A_{i}},\rho_{A_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tq^{T}_{i},\mu_{q^{T}_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tq_{i},\mu_{q_{i}})}

-

3.11
Oplax cocone of pseudo-algebras.

Now suppose that arbitrary bicoequalizers of codescent objects exist in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
and denote as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxcolim}}}\;A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(C,c,(\gamma^{t},\gamma^{s}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p,\pi)}

the bicoequalizer of the codescent object
\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{A}}
.
Then by pseudofunctoriality of bicoequalizer of codescent objects, and using that each pseudo-algebra is the bicoequalizer of its own bar construction, the morphism of codescent object
\overline{q_{i}}
at each
i
of
I
induces a canonical 2-cell in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TA_{i},\mu_{A_{i}},(\xi_{A_{i}},\rho_{A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(C,c,(\gamma^{t},\gamma^{s}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tq_{i},\mu_{q_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{i},\alpha_{i}^{s})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p,\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(l_{i},\lambda_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{i}\atop\simeq}

Proposition 3.12
.

The oplax cocone
(l_{i},\lambda_{i})_{i\in I}
is a an oplax bicolimit of
\mathbb{A}
in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
.

Proof.

Let be
(k_{i},\kappa_{i}):(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha_{i}^{s}))\rightarrow(B,b,(\beta^{t},\beta^{s}))
an oplax cocone over
\mathbb{A}
in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
. Its underlying oplax cocone
(k_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow B)_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{C}
provides us with a universal factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle k_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{i}\atop\simeq}

Applying and iterating the free construction to this diagram produces not only an invertible 2-cell in the category of algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TA_{i},\mu_{A_{i}},(\xi_{A_{i}},\rho_{A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TB,\mu_{B},(\xi_{B},\rho_{B}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tq_{i},\mu_{q_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tk_{i},\mu_{k_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\langle k_{i}\rangle_{i\in I},\mu_{\langle k_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\nu_{i}\atop\simeq}

but even a transformation of codescent object in
[\mathbb{X},T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}]_{\bf ps}
between the corresponding bar constructions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{X}_{(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i}))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{X}_{(B,b,(\beta^{t},\beta^{s}))}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{q_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{k_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\nu}_{i}\atop\simeq}

Then, the pseudofunctoriality of the bicoequalizer construction provides us with a prism whose vertical 1-cells are the bicoequalizing inclusions and the bottom face is a 2-cell relating induced 1-cells between the bicoequalizers. But we know by
proposition

2.9
that each
(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i}))
is the bicoequalizer of the corresponding
\mathscr{X}_{(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i}))}
, as well as
{(B,b,(\beta^{t},\beta^{s}))}
is the bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}_{(B,b,(\beta^{t},\beta^{s}))}
, and that each
(k_{i},\kappa_{i})
is induced from the morphism of codescent object it induces at the level of the overlying bar construction
\overline{k_{i}}
. Similarly the
(l_{i},\lambda_{i})
were induced by functoriality of the bicoequalizers from the morphism of codescent object induced by the
q_{i}
, while
(C,c,(\gamma^{t},\gamma^{s}))
was defined as the bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{A}}
. Hence we have the following equality of 2-cells

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TA_{i},\mu_{A_{i}},(\xi_{A_{i}},\rho_{A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(T\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i},\mu_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},(\xi_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}},\rho_{\underset{I}{{\textup{oplaxbicolim}}}\;A_{i}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A_{i},a_{i},(\alpha^{t}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(TB,\mu_{B},(\xi_{B},\rho_{B}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(C,c,(\gamma^{t},\gamma^{s}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,b,(\beta^{t},\beta^{s}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tq_{i},\mu_{q_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{i},\alpha_{i}^{s})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(k_{i},\kappa_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p,\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textup{bicoeq}}{(\overline{k})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Tk_{i},\mu_{k_{i}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(b,\beta^{s})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T\langle k_{i}\rangle_{i\in I},\mu_{\langle k_{i}\rangle_{i\in I}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{i}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\nu_{i}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi\atop\simeq}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

∎

Hence, from what precedes, assuming the existence of bicoequalizers in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
is sufficient to construct oplax bicolimits of pseudo-algebras thanks to the process above:

Corollary 3.13
.

Suppose that
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
has bicoequalizers of codescent objects. Then
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
has oplax bicolimits.

Finally, to conclude this section, recall that we saw at
theorem

1.19
that oplax bicolimits and bicoequalizers of codescent objects were sufficient to generate all bicolimits. A twofold use of codescent objects, once for constructing oplax-bicolimits and twice to correct them into actual bicolimits (thanks to a codescent diagram made of oplax bicolimits as in
lemma

1.15
), provides hence the following categorification of
[
Lin69
]
:

Theorem 3.14

(Linton theorem for pseudo-algebras)
.

Let be
(T,\eta,\mu,(\xi,\zeta,\rho))
a pseudomonad on a bicocomplete category
\mathcal{C}
. Then
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
is bicocomplete if and only if it has bicoequalizers of codescent objects.

4
The case of bifinitary pseudomonads

Now we come to the main section of this paper. It a famous result of monad theory that categories of algebras of finitary 2-monads are cocomplete: the strategy, due to
[
BW00
]
, consists in proving existence of coequalizers of algebras in an indirect way, by exhibiting a weakly initial coequalizing object through a filtered bicolimit in the underlying category, using the fact that this latter is preserved by the monad. Here we shall consider the case of
bifinitary
pseudomonads, that are pseudomonads preserving
bifiltered bicolimits
in the sense of
[
DLO22
]
.

Recall first that, as stated at
lemma

2.7
, if a pseudomonad preserves a certain shape of bicolimit, then pseudo-algebras inherit those bicolimits and the forgetful functor preserves them. In particular we have the following:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
(T,\eta,\mu,(\xi,\zeta,\rho))
be a bifinitary pseudomonad on a 2-category
\mathcal{C}
with bifiltered bicolimits. Then
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
has bifiltered bicolimits and
U_{T}
creates them.

Now, we want to establish that, more generally, 2-categories of pseudo-algebras of a bifinitary pseudomonad are bicocomplete. We proved in section 1 that conical
\sigma
-bicolimits are enough to construct arbitrary weighted bicolimits. Then we saw that oplax bicolimits and bicoequalizers of codescent objects were enough to construct
\sigma
-bicolimits. In the previous section we saw that bicocompleteness of the 2-category of pseudo-algebras of a pseudomonad was equivalent to existence of bicoequalizers of codescent objects. Hence it suffices to establish that the 2-category of pseudo-algebras of a bifinitary pseudomonad have bicoequalizers of codescent objects to ensure bicocompletness. Again our proof will be inspired by the classics of monad theory, as
[
Bor94
]
[section 4.3].

4.2
Setting of the theorem.

In this section we fix
(T,\eta,\mu,(\xi,\zeta,\rho))
a bifinitary pseudomonad on a bicomplete 2-category
\mathcal{C}
with bifiltered bicolimits and bicoequalizers of codescent objects; again we denote as
U_{T}
the associated forgetful 2-functor. We take a codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}:\mathbb{X}\rightarrow T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
of pseudo-algebras, with vertices and arrows denoted as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,a,(\alpha^{t},\alpha^{s}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,b,(\beta^{t},\beta^{s}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(C,c,(\gamma^{t},\gamma^{s}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f,\phi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(h,\psi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g,\chi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s,\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,\tau)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i,\iota)}

Now, considering the weight we give at
remark

1.7
, we obtain a 2-functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf Cat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathbb{X}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}][\mathcal{J},T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}[\mathscr{X},-]]}

and exhibiting a
\mathcal{J}
-weighted bicolimit of
\mathscr{X}
(that is, a bicoequalizer for
\mathscr{X}
) amounts to prove this 2-functor to be birepresentable.

4.3
Birepresentability and solution set.

In
[
BG88
]
[Theorem 3.5] it is shown that a 2-functor
T:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
with
\mathcal{A}
bicomplete (having all weighted bilimits) is birepresentable if and only the the 2-category
\int T
of elements of
T
has a small
weakly co-initiall
pseudofully faithful sub-2-category
\mathcal{H}\hookrightarrow\int T
(see
[
BG88
]
[Definition 3.1]), that is satisfying the condition that for any
A
in
\mathcal{A}
there exists some 1-cell
H\rightarrow A
with
H
in
\mathcal{H}
. Beware that
\mathcal{A}
needs to be bicomplete for this result to hold – for the representation will be constructed through bilimits.

Getting back to our situation, this tells us that to construct a bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}
, that is, a birepresentation of
[\mathbb{X}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}][\mathcal{J},T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}[\mathscr{X},-]]
, it suffices to exhibit such a small co-initial family in
\int[\mathbb{X}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}][\mathcal{J},T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}[\mathscr{X},-]]
. As in the 1-categorical case, the transfinite construction we perform here will actually produce such a co-initial family consisting of a
single
object (the bifiltered bicolimit of some directed chain) which will possess this weak initialness property. Beware from above that
\mathcal{C}
has to be bicomplete – in order
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
to be so by
proposition

2.6
to make use of
[
BG88
]
.

4.4
Initialization step: lower codescent data.

We shall construct a transfinite sequence of codescent diagrams. The first one, which will be hereafter denoted
\mathscr{X}_{0}
, is constructed as follows from the data above. First take the bicoequalizer in
\mathcal{C}
of the underlying codescent object
U_{T}\mathscr{X}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}={\textup{bicoeq}}(U_{T}\mathscr{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}

with
\kappa_{0}:q_{0}s\simeq q_{0}t
its inserted invertible 2-cell.

Then take the bicoequalizer of the free construction over the previous codescent diagram
TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}={\textup{bicoeq}}(TU_{T}\mathscr{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}

with
\pi_{0}:p_{0}Ts\simeq p_{0}Tt
its inserted invertible 2-cell. First, it is clear that the image
Tq_{0}
also pseudocoequalizes
TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
: whence a canonical map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}={\textup{bicoeq}}(TU_{T}\mathscr{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{0}\atop\simeq}

For the second map, first observe that we can use the structure maps
a,b,c
and the pseudomorphism structure over the morphism in
\mathscr{X}
to construct a morphism of codescent object
\overline{x}:TU_{T}\mathscr{X}\Rightarrow U_{T}\mathscr{X}
in
[\mathscr{X},\mathcal{C}]_{{\bf ps}}
. But for
P_{0}
and
Q_{0}
are the respective bicoequalizer of those codescent diagrams, this induces a canonical map
q_{0}={\textup{bicoeq}}(x)
together with a canonical 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{0}\atop\simeq}

This determines the lower data: set
\mathscr{X}_{0}(0)=TQ_{0}
,
\mathscr{X}_{0}(1)=TP_{0}
, while as in the 1-dimensional case, the lower projections are to be taken as
T(u_{0})
and
\mu_{Q_{0}}T(v_{0})
. To construct the common pseudo-section, we can again exhibit a morphism of codescent diagrams
\overline{i}:U_{T}\mathscr{X}\Rightarrow TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
and applying pseudofunctoriality of the bicoequalizer construction to provide a unique arrow
i_{0}=\langle p_{0}\eta_{C}\rangle
together with a canonical 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{0}\atop\simeq}

The unit for the lower data of our desired codescent diagram will be then chosen as
Ti_{0}
.

4.5
Initialization step : higher codescent data.

To determine the higher data, we can observe that our situation is very similar to the situation of
lemma

3.7
: here the analogy is obtained by replacing the computation of oplax bicolimit by the computation of the pseudocoequalizer; the combinations of data coming from the pseudo-algebraic structure, the multiplication and the comparision maps follows the same pattern. The higher object is obtained as the free construction over the bicoequalizer of the twice iterated free construction at the codescent diagram. Step by step, define
R_{0}
as the following bicoequilizer

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}={\textup{bicoeq}}(TTU_{T}\mathscr{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}

We are going to generate several maps
TR_{0}\rightarrow TP_{0}
from this universal property.

For the first one, just observe that
Tp_{0}
pseudocoqualizes
TTU_{T}\mathscr{X}
since
p_{0}
pseudocoequalizes
TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
by definition. This induces a first comparison map
t_{0}=\langle Tp_{0}\rangle
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}={\textup{bicoeq}}(TTU_{T}\mathscr{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tp_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{0}\atop\simeq}

Then one can take the following composite as the first higher map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{P_{0}}}

For the next map, observe that as above the data of the
Ta,Tb,Tc
define a morphism of codescent diagram
\overline{Tx}:TTU_{T}\mathscr{X}\Rightarrow TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
, inducing a morphism between the corresponding bicoequalizers
w_{0}=\langle p_{0}Tc\rangle
together with a universal invertible 2-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{0}\atop\simeq}

Similarly, the data of the multiplications
\mu_{A},\mu_{B},\mu_{C}
define a morphism of codescent diagrams
\overline{m}:TTU_{T}\mathscr{X}\Rightarrow TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
inducing a morphism
m_{0}=\langle p_{0}\mu_{C}\rangle
and a universal invertible 2-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{0}\atop\simeq}

Lemma 4.6
.

The following diagram, which we shall denote
\mathscr{X}_{0}
, defines a codescent object:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tm_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tw_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{P_{0}}Tt_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ti_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}Tv_{0}}

Proof.

We have to guess invertible 2-cells between the correct combinations of the higher and lower codescent data. All of them will be constructed thanks to some corresponding invertible 2-cells in
[\mathbb{X},\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
and then applying pseudofunctoriality of the bicoequalizer construction, though some will also involve further calculations.

One can check we have the following invertible 2-cell in
[\mathbb{X},\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TU_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{n_{0}}\atop\simeq}

induced from the triangle 2-cells of the pseudo-algebra structure
\alpha^{t}
,
\beta^{t}
,
\gamma^{t}
(where
\overline{w}
was constructed in
4.4
). Applying the pseudofunctor
{\textup{bicoeq}}(-)
returns hence a 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n_{0}\atop\simeq}

and it suffices then to take
Tn_{0}
as the first pseudo-retraction corresponding to
n_{0}
of
1.4
.

For the second retraction, we saw we had a morphism of codescent object
\overline{i}:U_{T}\mathscr{X}\rightarrow TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
: but observe that
[2,[\mathscr{X},\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}]_{\bf ps}\simeq[\mathscr{X},[2,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}]_{\bf ps}
: hence
\overline{i}
also defines a codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{i}
in
[2,\mathcal{C}]
. But on the other hand, the following invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{q_{0}}\atop\simeq}

is pseudocoequalizing for
\mathscr{X}_{i}
: for the bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}_{i}
in
[2,\mathcal{C}]
coincides with the universal 2-cell from which we induced
i_{0}
and
\iota_{0}
at the end of
4.4
, its universal property implies the existence of a unique 2-cell
\nu
satisfying the equations below

Then it suffices to paste
T\nu_{0}
together with the free canonical triangular 2-cell as below to get the desired retraction corresponding to
n_{1}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ti_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\nu_{0}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{Q_{0}}\atop\simeq}

We turn now to the higher composition. First, one can check we have the following invertible 2-cell in
[\mathscr{X},\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
induced from the square 2-cells of the pseudo-algebra structure
\alpha^{s}
,
\beta^{s}
,
\gamma^{s}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTU_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TU_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TU_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{T}\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Tx}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\theta}\atop\simeq}

Again, applying
{\textup{bicoeq}}(-)
returns an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta\atop\simeq}

and one just has to take
T\theta
as the first desired higher 2-cell corresponding to
\theta_{01}
.

Now observe again that the morphism of codescent object
\overline{Tx}
can also be seen as a codescent object
\mathscr{X}_{Tx}
in
[2,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
, whose bicoequalizer is given by the pair
(w_{0},\omega_{0})
as defined in
4.5
. But the pseudosquare
T\xi_{0}
(where
\xi_{0}
was defined at
4.4
), seen as a morphism in
[2,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
, pseudocoequalizes this codescent object
\mathscr{X}_{Tx}
as
\xi_{0}
was itself the bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}_{x}
, the codescent object associated to
\overline{x}
in
[2,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
: hence we have a 2-cell
\langle T\xi_{0}\rangle
as below satisfying the following equation

Now take the following pasting as the second higher 2-cell corresponding to
\theta_{02}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tw_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle T\xi_{0}\rangle\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u_{0}}\atop\simeq}

For the last 2-cell observe that the following pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tp_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTq_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tq_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{q_{0}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{0}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\upsilon_{0}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{0}^{-1}\atop\simeq}

induces by functoriality of bicoequalizers the following 2-cell
\tau

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{0}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau\atop\simeq}

and now we can take as the last codescent 2-cell, corresponding to
\theta_{12}
, the following pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tt_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tm_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\tau\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{TQ_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{Q_{0}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{v_{0}}\atop\simeq}

∎

We have hence constructed a first codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{0}
from the data of
\mathscr{X}
. Let us now turn to the induction step:

4.7
Codescent diagram at an induction step.

Suppose that the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{n}
is given for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, consisting of the data

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{P_{n}}Tt_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tw_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tm_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ti_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}Tv_{n}}

with the different arrows
u_{n}:P_{n}\rightarrow Q_{n}
,
v_{n}:P_{n}\rightarrow TQ_{n}
,
w_{n}:R_{n}\rightarrow P_{n}
,
t_{n}:R_{n}\rightarrow TP_{n}
and
m_{n}:R_{n}\rightarrow P_{n}
being given at the induction step; suppose moreover, for
n\geq 1
, that it is related to its predecessor step through the condition that
v_{n}=Tu_{n}T\eta_{Q_{n-1}}
(for the corresponding relation will be established at the next step later on). We must now construct a new codescent object
\mathscr{X}_{n+1}
from the data above.

4.8
Induction step: lower data.

First define
Q_{n+1}
and
u_{n+1}
as the bicoequalizer

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}={\textup{bicoeq}}({\mathscr{X}_{n}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}

and set the following identity
P_{n+1}=TQ_{n}
, and take
v_{n+1}=Tq_{n+1}
where the next
q_{n+1}
is defined as the composite
q_{n+1}=u_{n+1}\eta_{Q_{n}}
: that is we take
v_{n+1}
as the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{n+1}=TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}\eta_{Q_{n}}}

4.9
Common pseudosection - first step.

To construct the common pseudosection, we need to get back to the step
n-1
(we suppose here
n\geq 1
, addressing the case
n=1
in a further remark) to extract intermediate comparison maps from which we will induce
i_{n+1}
through a sequence of universal properties. It would be tempting to use directly
T\eta_{Q_{n}}
to induce
i_{n+1}
through the universal of
u_{n+1}
, but the unit by itself fails to pseudocoequalize the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{n}
. We must fist compose it with a map that is both expected to vanish when we shall achieve the transfinite induction, but also ensure pseudocoequalization.

As a first step, if both
\mathscr{X}_{n-1}
and
\mathscr{X}_{n}
have been defined in such a way that
v_{n}=Tu_{n}T\eta_{Q_{n-1}}
, then observe that
v_{n}
pseudocoequalizes
\mathscr{X}_{n-1}
thanks to the whiskering
T\xi_{n}*T\eta_{Q_{n-1}}
. As a consequence, the universal property of
u_{n}
, which is the pseudocoequalizing 1-cell of
\mathscr{X}_{n-1}
, ensures the existence of a canonical invertible 1-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TT{Q_{n-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{n}\atop\simeq}

Lemma 4.10
.

For any
n\geq 1
, the 2-cell
\mu_{Q_{n}}T\overline{v_{n}}
pseudocoequalizes the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{n}
.

Proof.

Consider the following pasting:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{Qn}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTTu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{TQ_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{Q_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\nu_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{TQ_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTT\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\nu_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{u_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\nu_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\zeta_{TQ_{n}}\atop\simeq}

∎

4.11
Common pseudosection - second step.

From
lemma

4.10
, the arrow
T\overline{v_{n}}
pseudocoequalizes the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{n}
; then so does its post-composition with
\mu_{Q_{n}}
, inducing hence a factorization through its bicoequalizer
u_{n+1}
as below, which we can take as the desired
i_{n+1}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}=P_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{i_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{n}\atop\simeq}

4.12
Induction step: higher data.

Concerning higher data, one must now declare
R_{n+1}=TP_{n}
. Now we construct the following higher maps as follows:

-

the
n
th step gives us a map
u_{n}:P_{n}\rightarrow Q_{n}
, whence
Tu_{n}:R_{n+1}=TP_{n}\rightarrow P_{n+1}=TQ_{n}
. Then takes simply
w_{n+1}=Tu_{n}
, and then its image along
T
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n}}

-

we also have the composite
\mu_{Q_{n}}Tv_{n}:R_{n+1}=TP_{n}\rightarrow P_{n+1}=TQ_{n}
. Then define
t_{n+1}=Tv_{n}
and take its image along
T
followed by the multiplication:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{TQ_{n}}Tt_{n+1}}

-

for the last one we use the multiplication of
Q_{n}
to determine
m_{n+1}=\mu_{Q_{n}}Tv_{n}
: then take the map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n+1}=TTQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tm_{n+1}}

Lemma 4.13
.

Those data define altogether a codescent object we can take as
\mathscr{X}_{n+1}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n+1}}Tv_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tw_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ti_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tm_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{P_{n+1}}Tt_{n+1}}

Proof.

We have to exhibit the invertible 2-cells corresponding to the coherence data. First, we must construct the invertible 2-cells exhibiting
Ti_{n+1}
as a common pseudosection of
Tu_{n+1}
and
\mu_{Q_{n+1}}Tv_{n+1}
. For the first one, consider the pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n-1}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{n+1}=TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\nu_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{n}\atop\simeq}

This pasting inserts an invertible 2-cell
\alpha:u_{n+1}i_{n+1}u_{n+1}Tu_{n}\Rightarrow u_{n+1}Tu_{n}
. But we know from the assumption that
\mathscr{X}_{n}
is a codescent object that
Tu_{n}
admits a pseudosection
Ti_{n}
: hence by general properties of pseudoretracts,
Tu_{n}
is co-fully faithful, hence the 2-cell
\alpha
comes uniquely from a 2-cell
\alpha^{\prime}:u_{n+1}i_{n+1}u_{n+1}\Rightarrow u_{n+1}
, and then one can use the universal property of
u_{n+1}
as the bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}_{n}
to retrieve from
\alpha^{\prime}
the desired 2-cell
u_{n+1}i_{n+1}\simeq 1_{Q_{n+1}}
corresponding to
n_{0}
of
1.4
.

For the second one, we precompose again
\mu_{Q_{n+1}}T_{v_{n+1}}Ti_{n+1}
with another combination of canonical, this time with the sequence
\eta_{Q_{n+1}}u_{n+1}\eta_{Q_{n}}u_{n}\eta_{Q_{n+1}}
and construct a first invertible 2-cell as the following pasting:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ti_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TTQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{v_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{i_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{T\eta_{Q_{n}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Tu_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\eta_{Q_{n}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\overline{v_{n}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{T\eta_{Q_{n}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{u_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\eta_{Q_{n}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\eta_{Q_{n-1}}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{Q_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{Q_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{u_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Tu_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}

Then one use the universal properties of the maps in the precomposite part, either as units or as bicoequalizers, to infer the existence of the desired 2-cell
\mu_{Q_{n+1}}TTu_{n+1}TT\eta_{Q_{n}}Ti_{n+1}\Rightarrow 1_{TQ_{n+1}}
corresponding to the
n_{1}
of
1.4
.

Now let us construct the higher coherence data. The first one is provided by applying
T
to the bicoequalizing 2-cell provided with
u_{n+1}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TR_{n+1}=TTP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n+1}=TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTv_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\xi_{n+1}\atop\simeq}

For the second 2-cell, we first construct the following auxiliary 2-cell we will call
\upsilon_{n}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{P_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{u_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{Q_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{v_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{n+1}\atop\simeq}

Now we construct the second higher coherence 2-cell by pasting the previously constructed 2-cell together with the component of the multiplication at
u_{n+1}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n+1}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{TQ_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{u_{n+1}}\atop\simeq}

Finally the last 2-cell is obtained by pasting the following structures 2-cells associated to the multiplication and their images along
T

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTTQ_{n}}}

∎

Remark 4.14
.

The construction of the step
\mathscr{X}_{1}
is actually covered by the generic recurrence step, except that the composite
Tu_{1}T\eta_{Q_{0}}
should not be called
v_{0}
for this later name was already used.

4.15
Transfinite induction.

We hence have at each
n\in\mathbb{N}
a codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{n}
which we would like to see as the bar construction of a pseudo-algebras: but it is not yet true for a finite
n
, as the bicoequalizer of this step only is the next
Q_{n+1}
rather than
Q_{n}
itself. However this stabilizes after iterating the construction
\omega
times. Those last steps are essentially the same as in 1-dimension, but for the sake of completeness and clarity we recall them.

The direct sequence
(Q_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is trivially a bifiltered diagram: hence we can compute in
\mathcal{C}
its bifiltered bicolimit
Q_{\omega}={\textup{bicolim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\,Q_{n}
. Moreover, observing that the set of all
\{n+1\mid n\in\mathbb{N}\}
is cofinal in
\mathbb{N}
we have

P_{\omega}=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\;P_{n+1}=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\;TQ_{n}\simeq T\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\;Q_{n}=TQ_{\omega}

But now, the transition maps
u_{n+1}:P_{n+1}=TQ_{n}\rightarrow Q_{n+1}
induces a structure map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\omega}\simeq TQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}

Similarly, one has

R_{\omega}=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\;R_{n+1}=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\;TP_{n}\simeq T\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{bicolim}}}\;P_{n}=TP_{\omega}=TTQ_{\omega}

Moreover one can check that the map
Tv_{n}:TP_{n}\rightarrow TTQ_{n}
is sent by the bicolimit to the identity
1_{R_{\omega}}
because it is induced from the transition morphisms.

Let us describe also what becomes of the higher and lower data of the codescent diagrams
\mathscr{X}_{n}
after the transfinite induction. In each case, we infer this from the expression of the
n+1
step for a given
n\geq 1
, using again the same cofinality argument. While we saw that
u_{n}
induces a structure map
u_{\omega}
above, we can tell that the bicolimit arrow of the
(v_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is the identity of
P_{\omega}
for
v_{n+1}
: we are going to prove indeed at
lemma

4.16

u_{\omega}
to be a pseudoretraction of
\eta_{Q_{\omega}}
, but we know that
v_{n+1}=T(u_{n+1}\eta_{Q_{n}})
, which will after transfinite induction induce an invertible 2-cell
v_{\omega}\simeq 1_{P_{\omega}}
. As to their common section, it will be
T\eta_{Q_{\omega}}
. The higher data will be determined the same way: we will use
w_{\omega}=Tu_{\omega}
,
\mu_{P_{\omega}}
(as
t_{\omega}=Tv_{\omega}\simeq 1_{R_{\omega}}
), and
m_{\omega}=\mu_{Q_{\omega}}
. We end up with the following codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{\omega}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTTQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{TQ_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTu_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mu_{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\eta_{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{\omega}}}

Lemma 4.16
.

The map
u_{\omega}
defines a structure of pseudo-algebra on
Q_{\omega}
.

Proof.

We exhibit triangle and square data forming together with
u_{\omega}
a structure of pseudo-algebra. For the triangle, recall that we defined at each
n\in\mathbb{N}
the transition map
q_{n+1}
as the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}=P_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Then, for
{\textup{bicolim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{n}\simeq{\textup{bicolim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{n+1}
by cofinality, we end up with the following triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{\omega}=P_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{t}\atop\simeq}

For the square data, one has at each
n\in\mathbb{N}
an inserted 2-cell from the bicoequalizing property of
u_{n+1}:TQ_{n}\rightarrow Q_{n+1}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{n+1}=TP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}=P_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}=P_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{n}\atop\simeq}

This 2-cell is sent by the bicolimit functor to an invertible 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{\omega}=TP_{\omega}=TTQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{\omega}=P_{{\omega}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{\omega}=P_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\omega}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{\omega}}}

and it suffices to take
u^{s}=\xi_{\omega}
. Then
(Q_{\omega},u_{\omega},(u^{t},u^{s}))
is a pseudoalgebra.
∎

Remark 4.17
.

One can check that the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{\omega}
is the underlying diagram of the bar construction at
(Q_{\omega},u_{\omega},(u^{t},u^{s}))
, that is

\mathscr{X}_{\omega}=U_{T}\mathscr{X}_{(Q_{\omega},u_{\omega},(u^{t},u^{s}))}

This is not a coincidence but the very aim of the way the diagrams
\mathscr{X}_{n}
were designed. The first bicoequalizer
Q_{0}
of
\mathscr{X}
does not yet bear a structure of pseudo-algebra, though being equipped with different structure maps that could be assembled in a codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{0}
close to be a bar construction. This situation reproduced at each recurrence step, with in particular the transition map
q_{n+1}:Q_{n}\rightarrow Q_{n+1}
measuring the failure of
Q_{n}
to be the bicoequalizer of the bar-like construction
\mathscr{X}_{n}
, and at the same time, of
u_{n+1}
to define a structure of pseudo-algebra. Eventual stabilisation at the transfinite step simultaneously corrects those two defects at once.

4.18
Canonical pseudomorphism.

Now each for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have not only a bicolimit inclusion
q^{\omega}_{0}:Q_{n}\rightarrow Q_{\omega}
but also a bicolimit inclusion in
[2,\mathcal{C}]_{\bf ps}
of the arrows
u_{n}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\omega}=TQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\omega}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\omega}_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\omega}_{n}\atop\simeq}

In particular, composing the inclusion at
0
with the canonical 2-cell obtained at
4.4
gives us the following 2-cell which is the desired pseudomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TC}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\omega}=TQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\omega}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\omega}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{0}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\omega}_{0}\atop\simeq}

In the following we shall denote it as
(q_{\omega},\kappa_{\omega})
. It is routine, yet tedious, to check that this defines a pseudomorphism.

Proposition 4.19
.

The map
(q_{\omega},\kappa_{\omega}):(C,c,(\gamma^{s},\gamma^{t}))\rightarrow(Q_{\omega},u_{\omega},(u^{s},u^{t}))
pseudocoequalizes the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}
in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
. Moreover, any other data
(l,\lambda):(C,c,(\gamma^{s},\gamma^{t}))\rightarrow(D,d,(\delta^{s},\delta^{t}))
pseudocoequalizing
\mathscr{X}
admits a factorization through
(q_{\omega},\kappa_{\omega})
.

Proof.

The argument is similar to
[
Bor94
]
. First, it is clear that this arrow
(q_{\omega},\kappa_{\omega})
pseudocoequalizes
\mathscr{X}
because it factorizes in
[2,\mathcal{C}]
through the bicoequalizer of
\overline{x}:TU_{T}\mathscr{X}\rightarrow U_{T}\mathscr{X}
(see
4.4
).

Now take a pseudomorphism
(l,\lambda):(C,c,(\gamma^{s},\gamma^{t}))\rightarrow(D,d,(\delta^{s},\delta^{t}))
pseudocoequalizing
\mathscr{X}
. Then the underlying
l:C\rightarrow D
pseudocoequalizes
U_{T}\mathscr{X}
while
Tl
also bicoequalizes
TU_{T}\mathscr{X}
: we have then a canonical 2-cell satisfying the equation below
