Now suppose you have a factorization of
(l,\lambda)
trough a 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TD}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle Tl\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle l\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\lambda\rangle_{n}\atop\simeq}

Then we must show that
dT\langle l\rangle_{n}
pseudocoequalizes the codescent diagram
\mathscr{X}_{n}
. The inserted 2-cell will be constructed as the pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TP_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TTQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TTD}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TD}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TD}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tu_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tv_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle l\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle l\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TT\langle l\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Td}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{s}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\langle l\rangle_{n}}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle Tl\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\nu_{n}\atop\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle\lambda\rangle_{n}\atop\simeq}

where
\nu_{n}
can be obtained from the universal property of the unit and bicoequalizer of codescent object hidden in
v_{n}
. Once one has proven this 2-cell to satisfy the coherence conditions, we are provided with a canonical 2-cell which is the next factorization posting
\langle Tl\rangle_{n+1}=T\langle l\rangle_{n}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{n}=P_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TD}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle l\rangle_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle l\rangle_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\lambda\rangle_{n+1}\atop\simeq}

Having constructed such a factorization for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we can induce an ultimate factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{TQ_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TD}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q_{\omega}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\langle l\rangle_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle l\rangle_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\lambda\rangle_{\omega}\atop\simeq}

which is the desired 2-cell establishing the factorization of
(l,\lambda)
through
(q_{\omega},k_{\omega})
.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

In fact one can check that the factorization provided by
(\langle l\rangle_{\omega},\langle\lambda\rangle_{\omega})
is a morphism in the 2-category of elements
[\mathbb{X}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}][\mathcal{J},T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}[\mathscr{X},-]]
, that is, is a morphism between pseudocoequalizing data for
\mathscr{X}
.

Corollary 4.21
.

The category
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
has bicoequalizers of codescent objects.

Proof.

We shown at
proposition

4.19
that
(q_{\omega},\kappa_{\omega}):(C,c,(\gamma^{s},\gamma^{t}))\rightarrow(Q_{\omega},u_{\omega},(u^{s},u^{t}))
provides a single-object co-initial family in the 2-category of elements of
[\mathbb{X}^{{\rm op}},{\bf Cat}][\mathcal{J},T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}[\mathscr{X},-]]:T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}\rightarrow{\bf Cat}
. Moreover,
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
has bilimits inherited from
\mathcal{C}
as established at
proposition

2.6
, which are clearly preserved by this 2-functor from its expression. Hence, as explained in
4.3
,
[
BG88
]
[Theorem 3.5] ensures hence that this 2-functor is birepresentable: the representing object is the desired bicoequalizer of
\mathscr{X}
.
∎

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
(T,\eta,\mu,(\xi,\zeta,\rho))
be a bifinitary pseudomonad on a bicomplete and bicocomplete 2-category
\mathcal{C}
. Then the 2-category of pseudo-algebras and pseudomorphisms
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
is bicomplete and bicocomplete.

Proof.

First, bicompleteness is inherited from
\mathcal{C}
as established at
proposition

2.6
. Hence
From
proposition

4.19
, bicoequalizers exist in
T\mbox{-}{\bf psAlg}
. But from
theorem

3.14
, this ensures existence of oplax bicolimits, and thus of arbitrary bicolimits thanks to
theorem

1.19
.
∎

We finish with a list of examples, which are also investigated in further details in
[
DLO22
]
:

Example 4.23
.

Recall that the 2-category
{\bf Lex}
of small lex categories and lex functors is the 2-category of pseudo-algebras for the (strict) KZ-monad
{\bf Lex}[-]
on
{\bf Cat}
sending a small category on its free completion under finite limits. In
[
DLO22
]
[proposition 5.3.2] we prove the KZ-monad
{\bf Lex}[-]
to be bifinitary. For
{\bf Cat}
is bicocomplete, so is
{\bf Lex}
by
theorem

4.22
.

Example 4.24
.

In the same reference
[
DLO22
]
[section 5.4] we investigate 2-categories of
\Phi
-exact categories
for a class of finite weights
\Phi
- see
[
GL12
]
for the general theory of
lex colimits
. The 2-category of
\Phi
-exact categories and
\Phi
-exact functors is the 2-category of pseudo-algebras of a pseudomonad
\Phi_{l}
on
{\bf Lex}
. In
[
DLO22
]
[Lemma 5.4.4] we prove the pseudomonad
\Phi_{l}
to be bifinitary. As
{\bf Lex}
is bicocomplete, so is the 2-category
\Phi\mbox{-}\textbf{Ex}
of
\Phi
-exact categories. This includes the following examples of 2-categories, which are hence bicocomplete:

-

Reg
, the 2-category of small regular categories and regular functors;

-

Ex
, the 2-category of small (Barr)-exact categories and exact functors;

-

Coh
, the 2-category of small coherent categories and coherent functors;

-

\textbf{Ext}_{\omega}
, the 2-category of small finitely-extensive categories and functors preserving finite coproducts;

-

Adh
, the 2-category of small adhesive categories and adhesive functors;

-

\textbf{Pretop}_{\omega}
, the 2-category of small finitary pretopoi and coherent functors.
